About this text

The Higher Tantra of the Abhidhāna, known in Sanskrit as the Abhidhānottara Tantra and in Tibetan as Ngönpar Jöpé Gyü Lama, is a scripture of the Cakrasaṃvara cycle of Buddhist tantra, opening with homage to glorious Vajraḍāka and set among the wrathful vajra beings, ḍākas and ḍākinīs of all the tathāgatas. Over many chapters it presents the deities of the Heruka and Cakrasaṃvara maṇḍalas, their seed syllables, mantras, colors and hand emblems, along with rites, the sacred places of pilgrimage understood as present within the body, and the practice of meditating on oneself as the deity in union with wisdom and means. Its closing chapters stress that the outer forms of worship are surpassed by inner yoga and by meditation on emptiness and nondual wisdom. It is Toh 369 in the Kangyur, in the Tantra section, where it belongs to the explanatory tantras of the Cakrasaṃvara literature, and it was translated into Tibetan by Dīpaṃkaraśrījñāna and Rinchen Zangpo and later revised by others.

Translation

In the language of India: a bhi dha na utta ra tantra nā ma. In the language of Tibet: The Higher Tantra of the Abhidhāna.

Homage to the Blessed One, glorious Vajraḍāka.

As far as the ends of the six classes of beings,
all the buddhas come forth;
this is the work of Vajrasattva.
To the dharmadhātu I bow and offer praise.

Glorious Vajraḍāka,
embodiment of the three bodies and the five wisdoms,
dwelling in the wheel of the ḍākinīs,
liberator of beings, to you I bow and offer praise.

For as long as the vajra ḍākinīs
cut the bonds of conceptual thought
and fully engage in the work of the world,
for that long I always bow and offer praise.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One was dwelling in the secret essence of the vajra wrathful ones, the ḍākas and the ḍākinīs of all tathāgatas. There the blessed ones, all the tathāgatas, made a request to the Blessed One, the beginningless Vajrasattva, and the Blessed One said: "I shall explain the essence, higher than the higher, of the Abhidhāna of the net of the vajra wrathful ones, ḍākas and ḍākinīs of all tathāgatas. Vajrapāṇi, lord of the great wrathful ones, I shall explain in detail the magical display with its diverse retinue. Listen well and hold it in mind."

At this he said, "Excellent, Blessed One," joined his palms and bowed, and the Blessed One spoke:

Now the secret is to be explained,
in brief and not at length.
Glorious Heruka, the perfect union,
accomplishes all aims that are desired.
Higher even than the higher
is this later Abhidhāna,
the binding of the net of ḍākinīs.
In the delight of the supreme secret
the self of all forever abides.
The ḍāka, the illusion, the sattva
is Vajrasattva, the supreme bliss.
This is the self-arisen blessed hero,
the binding of the net of ḍākas.

This great beginningless mode,
the abode in the bhaga where existence, sound and form arise,
is the field of conduct for those who practice the pledge.
Stainless in beginning, middle and end,
hard to find in the three worlds,
it is the perfect union of churner and churned.
For whomever, and in whatever way is fitting,
it is naturally pure and luminous,
arising from the secret place.
Ever free of faults and peaceful,
become equal to space,
it brings about emanation and withdrawal.
Naturally pure and self-arisen,
it bestows the bliss of the yoginī.
Endowed with recitation and meditative concentration,
and with the wisdom of the rite of union itself:
these are taught in the tantra.
Lord of Secrets, listen well.

The supreme ones, the breath-releasing and the intermediate,
together with fragrant water,
the wise one should make into pills
and offer them to the maṇḍalas of the retinue.
At the special time of their arising,
the innate is accomplished through the messenger.
Supreme, middling and lowest,
all accomplishments up to the ultimate arise.
The accomplishment of joy
that the mind desires will also be accomplished.
With drops of one's own substance
one should worship the buddhas and bodhisattvas.
Through the union of one's own deity
one should worship oneself and others too.
By seeing, by touching,
by hearing and by remembering,
one is freed from all wrongdoing;
of this there is no doubt.
The yogin himself is supreme merit,
the purifier who destroys wrongdoing.
Through pure recitation and meditative concentration too,
accomplishment comes in this very life.

From the Higher Tantra of the Abhidhāna, higher than the higher, this is the first chapter, teaching the hidden secret of entering the pledge.

Then the Blessed One Vajrasattva entered into equipoise in the samādhi of the great wrathful one called Magical Display in the Dharmadhātu, and taught the secret of all accomplishments to bodhisattva great beings as numerous as the atoms of countless buddhafields, unattached and free of attachment, namely: the great hero called Form Vajra, the great hero called Feeling Vajra, the great hero called Perception Vajra, the great hero called Formations Vajra, the great hero called Consciousness Vajra, the great Vajra Maheśvara, chief of all the tathāgatas of the dharmadhātu, the great hero Delusion Vajra, the great hero Ear Vajra, the great hero Nose Vajra, the great hero Attachment Vajra, the great hero Jealousy Vajra, the great hero called Lord of All Tathāgatas, the great hero Earth Vajra, the great hero Water Vajra, the great hero Fire Vajra, the great hero Wind Vajra, and the great hero Space Vajra. With these and others like them, without exception and leaving none out, reaching to the limits of the dharmadhātu, infinite and radiating, through the arising of the secret essence, an assembly of the diverse wheels of the maṇḍala, he dwelt together with the wheel of the pledge of the three secrets: the great hero Vajra Khaṇḍakapāla, the great hero Vajra Mahākaṅkāla, the great hero Vajra Kaṅkāla, the great hero Vajra Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra, the great hero Vajra Suravairin, the great hero Vajra Amitābha, the great hero Vajraprabha, the great hero Vajradeha, the great hero Vajra Aṅkuraka, the great hero Vajra Jaṭila, the great hero Vajra Mahāvīra, the great hero Vajra Hūṃkāra, the great hero Vajra Subhadra, the great hero Vajrabhadra, the great hero Vajra Mahābhairava, the great hero Vajra Virūpākṣa, the great hero Vajra Mahābala, the great hero Vajraratna, the great hero Vajra Paramāśva, the great hero Vajra Ākāśagarbha, the great hero Vajra Śrī Mahāheruka, the great hero Vajra Padmanarteśvara, the great hero Vajra Vairocana, and the great hero Vajrasattva. All the realms of space were seen to be completely filled by heroes such as these and by all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, lord of all families, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Nonduality of the Pledge of the Three Vajras, and his own secret pledge, the yoginīs arising from the secret bhaga, and

taught the great pledge of union, the nondual secret pledge of the heroes, namely: the great yoginī called Pātanī, the great yoginī called Māraṇī, the great yoginī called Ākarṣaṇī, the great yoginī called Narteśvarī, the great yoginī called Padmajālinī; the great ḍākinī called Vajraḍākinī, the great ḍākinī called Vajralāmā, the great ḍākinī called Vajrakhaṇḍarohā, the great ḍākinī called Vajrarūpiṇī; the great yoginī called Pracaṇḍā, the great yoginī called Caṇḍākṣī, the great yoginī called Prabhāvatī, the great yoginī called Mahānāsā, the great yoginī called Vīramatī, the great yoginī called Kharvarī, the great yoginī called Laṅkeśvarī, the great yoginī called Drumacchāyā, the great yoginī called Airāvatī, the great yoginī called Mahābhairavā, the great yoginī called Vāyuvegā, the great yoginī called Surābhakṣī, the great yoginī called Śyāmādevī, the great yoginī called Subhadrā, the great yoginī called Hayakarṇā, the great yoginī called Khagānanā, the great yoginī called Cakravegā, the great yoginī called Khaṇḍarohā, the great yoginī called Śauṇḍinī, the great yoginī called Cakravarmiṇī, the great yoginī called Suvīrā, the great yoginī called Mahābalā, the great yoginī called Cakravartinī, and the great yoginī called Mahāvīryā. Heroes and yoginīs such as these, in all their variety, were seen to fill everything to the very limits of the realm of space, proclaiming the sounds hūṃ and phaṭ, performing many kinds of dance, embracing and kissing their own pledge partners, perfecting the taste of great compassion.

Having seen this, they entered, and the supreme heroes called to mind the pledge. The wisdom of the nondual pledge of glorious Vajraḍāka and the ḍākinī Vajravārāhī truly arose, and the Perfection of Wisdom arose.

Then the Blessed One taught the families and the secret of all the hosts of mātṛkās, namely: the vajra mātṛkā called Kākāsyā, the vajra mātṛkā called Ulūkāsyā, the vajra mātṛkā called Śvānāsyā, the vajra mātṛkā called Sūkarāsyā, the vajra mātṛkā called Yamadāḍhī, the vajra mātṛkā called Yamadūtī, the vajra mātṛkā called Yamadaṃṣṭrī, and the vajra mātṛkā called Yamamathanī. All the realms of space were filled by every quarter of such hosts of mātṛkās. The lord of the family, glorious Heruka, from whom arises the nondual wisdom whose nature is the pure dharmadhātu, embodiment of the Perfection of Wisdom, is the great pledge, the pledge of the vajra family:

All phenomena are without entity,
entirely free of every characteristic;
it truly arises from the selfless Dharma.
This is the supreme pledge.

Then the Blessed One taught the vajra pledge called the Nondual Vajra Armor, namely: the great lord of yoga Vajrasattva, the great lord of yoga Vairocana Vajra, the great lord of yoga Padmanarteśvara, the great lord of yoga Vajra Heruka, the great lord of yoga Vajra Sūrya, and the great lord of yoga Vajra Paramāśva. These six families are to be set upon the six limbs. That supreme pledge of nondual protection arises from the nonduality of method and wisdom.

I shall explain the hidden secret, supreme in the great yoga: the great vajra goddess Vajravārāhī, the great vajra goddess Yāminī, the great vajra goddess Mohanī, the great vajra goddess Sañcālinī, the great vajra goddess Santrāsanī, and the great vajra goddess Caṇḍikā. These too are the great secret mind of the union that perfectly protects the nondual pledge of the realm of space and of the whole dharmadhātu. Many yoginīs of such various kinds made offering with their own hands of supreme warm blood, joined their palms, and with supreme devotion touched their crowns to the ground.

Then he entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Great Pledge, Magical Display, the Variegated Vajra. From the pores of his own body he emanated and sent forth beings of every kind, and for the many to be trained he filled the whole dharmadhātu, without exception and leaving none out, with methods of every kind, and the sky in front was seen to be completely filled with this variety. The Blessed One, assuming the guise of the wrathful ones of great compassion of the various families, for the benefit of sentient beings displayed miracles of the various joys of nondual taste, as numerous as atoms, and the lord of the three realms spoke:

Upon water, fire, wind and the Mahendra earth is Sumeru, king of mountains, of many kinds, beautiful with the splendor of many kinds of jewels. Upon it glorious Vajrasattva drew forth the great vajra wrathful one from the dharmadhātu and emanated him; and when he had emanated the great vajra palace, king of great maṇḍalas, the great Samantabhadra arose, came forth together with the vajra retinue, and dwelt there firmly.

It was adorned with excellences of every kind, ornamented with ornaments of vajra jewels, lovely as the colors of the moon and sun, and patterned in a lattice design with nets of bells, yak-tail whisks, victory banners and small tinkling bells. It resounded with the sound of the thread of the vajra pledge entering the wheel of Dharma. It resounded with the cries of every kind of god, nāga, yakṣa, rākṣasa, bhūta, preta and piśāca, of unmādas, apasmāras and shadow-oppressing spirits, of the vicious and the exceedingly savage, of vetālas, corpses and burning corpses, of dogs, jackals and she-jackals. It resounded with the cries of "phem" and "alala" of heroes, ḍākas and ḍākinīs, and with the sound of bells and ḍamaru drums, and it was adorned with severed heads that danced.

He whose conduct is that of the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, who liberates through virtuous action and benefits all sentient beings with the taste of compassion; who, encircled by a whirling retinue, dances the lotus dance with joined palms, and stands with the right leg extended and the palms of the hands joined; who teaches the profound way of the supreme Dharma of the fragments of buddha skulls and the desire of the great king of wrathful ones; who holds a great body and proclaims a profound sound; who possesses the maṇḍala of his own retinue; from whom the great Highest Abhidhāna arises; who possesses the net of nets of the king of the vow of the ḍākas and ḍākinīs; the Teacher who teaches in accord with the various dharmas, who grants and teaches the bliss suited to the various intentions of sentient beings: "Great being, excellent, excellent! Vajrapāṇi, listen well!"

This concludes the second chapter, the chapter of the request, from the Abhidhāna Tantra, Highest of the High.

Then the Blessed One spoke the vajra secret essence to the whole maṇḍala of the retinue:

Having meditated on a moon at the heart,
The magical display of the subtle vajra,
The secret of all the sugatas,
Is the vow of the net of ḍākinīs.
There, blessed as the secret,
Listen well to the supreme essence:
The highest even of the highest,
The vow of the net of ḍākinīs.

oṃ svabhāvaśuddhāḥ sarvvadharmāḥ svabhāvaśuddho'haṃ oṃ śūnyatājñānavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ oṃ vajraśuddhāḥ sarvvadharmā vajraśuddho'haṃ oṃ yogaśuddhāḥ sarvvadharmā yogaśuddho'haṃ oṃ jñānaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā jñānaśuddho'haṃ oṃ samayaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā samayaśuddho'haṃ oṃ sarvatathāgataśūnyatāanurāgaṇavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ oṃ sarvatathāgataprakṛtasuviśuddhādharmadhātuvajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ oṃ mantraśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā mantraśuddho'haṃ oṃ krodhaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ krodhaśuddho'haṃ oṃ cittaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāścittaśuddho'haṃ oṃ vākśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā vākaśuddho'haṃ oṃ kāyaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ kāyaśuddho'haṃ oṃ jñānaamṛtaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā jñānaamṛtaśuddho'haṃ oṃ nānāsamayaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā nānāsamayaśuddho'haṃ oṃ cāryanayaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāryanayśuddho'haṃ oṃ triyogapariśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāstriyogapariśuddho'haṃ oṃ abhiṣekaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā abhiṣekaśuddho'haṃ oṃ pūjaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ pūjaśuddho'haṃ oṃ sarvatathāgataadvayapūjavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ maṇḍalaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā maṇḍalaśuddho'haṃ oṃ adhipatiśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā adhipatiśuddho'haṃ vīraśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā vīrśuddho'haṃ oṃ yoginiśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā yoginīśuddho'haṃ oṃ tatvaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāstatvaśuddho'haṃ he iti hetvapagataḥ sarvadharmā hetvapagato'haṃ ru iti rukārāpagataḥ sarvadharmā rukārāpagato'haṃ ka itinkvacitasthitaḥ sarvadharmā nakvacitasthito'haṃ oṃ jhaṭitakaraśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā jhaṭitakaraśuddho'haṃ oṃ bhavaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā bhavaśuddho'haṃ oṃ japaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā japaśuddho'haṃ oṃ āhāraśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā āhāraśuddho'haṃ oṃ bhakṣaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā bhakṣaśuddho'haṃ oṃ asamāhitaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā asahitśuddho'haṃ oṃ svasamavedanaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ svasambadenaśuddho'haṃ oṃ dveṣaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā dveṣaśuddho'haṃ oṃ mohaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā mohaśuddho'haṃ oṃ rāgaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā rāgaśuddho'haṃ oṃ mānaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā mānaśuddho'haṃ oṃ īrṣaśuddhaḥ sarvadharmā īrṣaśuddho'haṃ oṃ khavajraśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ khvajraśuddho'haṃ oṃ tricakrapariśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāstricakrapariśuddho'haṃ oṃ ḍākinīśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā ḍākinīśuddho'haṃ oṃ śaṅkulśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ śuṅkulaśuddho'haṃ oṃ mātriśuddhāḥ sarvadharmā mātriśuddho'haṃ oṃ pitṛśuddhaiḥ sarvadharmāḥ pitṛśuddho'haṃ

In this way, everything, as far as the secondary charnel grounds, should be known as completely pure.

pu ja au a go rā de mā ka o tri ko ka la ka hi pre gri sau su na si ma ku

The ten grounds, whose nature is complete purity,
And likewise the ten perfections,
And the ten knowledges, of purified nature:
Meditate fully on these tens.
Because the three wheels too are completely pure,
The twenty-four are virtuous in their nature.
Through liberation from times and lunar days,
The minds of the yoginīs rejoice.
The meaning of the thirty-seven purities
Arose from the exalted Heruka.
The Abhidhāna Tantra, highest of the high,
Is the sublime of the supreme great secret.

By circling to the left and to the right,
One should laugh, make offerings and circumambulate.
One who knows that reality
Should teach the five signs.
To anyone who has no empowerment,
Do not teach that reality in any other way.
In the Abhidhāna Tantra, the master
Gives the oral instruction to the empowered disciple.
Otherwise the disciple goes into madness,
Into fainting and stupor.
Into Avīci, the great terror,
That wicked one will fall.
One who gives the volume to one who is no friend,
That wicked one dies at once.
Boils, fevers, poison and disease:
He will be tormented by ḍāka grahas.
By savage obstructors and by Gaṇapati
He will be slain and fall into hell.
With every effort possible,
Master and disciple, in due order,
Having always kept that reality hidden,
Will swiftly gain the siddhi of mantra.
When the master, everything in due order,
According to the stages of the Abhidhāna Tantra,
Applies it to deity yoga,
Nondual wisdom will arise.
At once there is awakening;
The supreme will surely be found.
A human girl such as this should be avoided:
One who does not know my pledges,
Fierce, of wrong views, of wicked nature,
Savage and without compassion,
Possessed of greed and avarice,
Cunning, wicked and jealous,
Attached to other men,
And full of conceptual thought.
Such a mudrā should be avoided;
For her, the siddhi of union is far off.
Take a Pukkasī, lovely and beautiful,
Adorned with a beautiful form and youth,
Single-minded toward union,
Steady in her observance and knowing the pledges,
Brave, free of attachment, without shame,
Without pride and without avarice.
Mantrin, this is your own conduct:
You should take up the vidyā and the observance.
Always wear the five mudrās,
And make skull ornaments for your head as well.
Hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṃ,
Smeared all over with the dust of ashes.
Always eat mahāmāṃsa,
Be intoxicated by drinking everything,
Sit upon a corpse seat,
And wear a great hide as clothing.
Eat food that accords with this,
Be sated with fat and blood.
In villages or in wildernesses,
Whatever land one goes to,
One should never stay there out of attachment,
But always practice in charnel grounds,
Or else stay in the marketplace.
The śrāvakas, the unfortunate of the world,
Are pervaded by unknowing and delusion;
In a temple that the śrāvakas enjoy,
One should not stay.
Just like a poor man,
So the bodhisattva dwells.
He sports with the vajra dance,
Always dwelling in the charnel ground,
Eating excrement and great flesh as food,
Furnished with intoxicating drink.
The chief of heroes, with one-pointed
Mind, should meditate intently.
If he has not found the great mudrā,
That which has fully abandoned all distraction
Is the source of wisdom,
Arisen from the perfection of wisdom.
Not apprehending grasped and grasper,
Abide in nonduality itself.
Having made the mind one-pointed,
If one abides in the conduct of meditation,
The siddhi of delight in bliss will come.
The ritual sequence of wisdom and means:
Sport gently with the great mudrā.
Through meditation becoming manifest,
Liberation and all the buddhas too
Will be attained in this very life.
The left-hand conduct is wisdom;
The right, likewise, is means.
The might of one endowed with meditation
Holds sway over the lord of the Thirty (Indra).
Yoginīs and accomplished ones,
Daughters of yakṣas and of nāgas,
And various gandharvīs besides:
With them he enjoys the pleasures of desire.
That reality, the supreme secret:
Always meditate on the vowels and consonants.
Through the yoga of one's own deity,
Examine everything in the same way.
As for the signs the hero shows:
With the yogin's left hand
One should engage in the signs.
This, which has become the abode of heroes,
The three worlds, moving and unmoving,
All beings, have arisen from the left.
Vajrasattva is the thumb itself;
In the index finger Vairocana dwells;
In the middle finger abides Padmanarteśvara;
The ring finger is the hero possessing siddhi;
In the little finger, the Inexhaustible Body;
On the surface of the nails abides Hayagrīva.
Those that arise from the cause of the left
Are said to be all the heroes.
The palm is the supreme gathering of the messengers.
Having ascertained the back well,
The five signs should be shown.
Practitioner, understand this:
Earth is said to be Locanā;
The water element is said to be Māmakī;
Fire is said to be Pāṇḍaravāsinī;
Wind is known as Tārā.
She Who Kisses is emptiness,
And likewise she is the perfection.
All the heroes of the center
The wise one invites to go to the back.
This secret should not be shown;
With great effort it should be hidden.
Thus, by means of signs, the heroes
Eat food together with them.
The union of all by the hero
Is the Saṃvara of the net of ḍākas.
For all who follow one another,
I shall explain the various signs.
The characteristics of the ḍākinīs
And the characteristics of the messengers,
In order to benefit the practitioner,
All of these I shall explain.
Painted cloths and images:
When they are painted, likewise the pledges,
And the distinctions of body color,
By which the practitioner is accomplished;
The perfect maṇḍala,
Beautiful with various mantras;
The delightful pledges of the deities;
The yoginīs, various and supreme;
Of various delightful heroes
The yoginīs fully assembled,
The ḍākinīs too fully assembled,
Their own various families, supreme and sublime.
According to the word of the lineage of masters,
In order to benefit the disciple,
He is highly skilled in the activities of the lord,
Well trained in mantra and in tantra,
And moreover in the ritual sequences,
Skilled in the activities of the Highest Abhidhāna.
The mantrin examines the characteristics of the paśu,
The distinctions of the various families,
The conduct of the yoginīs,
And likewise of the ḍākinīs.
The outer mudrās of the mātṛkās
Arose from the family of Vārāhī.
The conduct of all the heroes
And the conduct of the cakra he has thoroughly learned.
In the characteristics and conduct
Of the outer lord of yoga he is skilled.
Arisen from the family of glorious Heruka,
He is trained in virtuous conduct.
In the distinctions of conduct in the lands
And in the conduct of a master he is skilled.
The practice of the yoginī tantras,
The vast yoga tantras,
The sequence of the divisions of the kriyā tantras:
All the tantras he fully knows.
The scriptures and treatises of accomplishment, and

The sūtras and likewise the jātakas,
The unsurpassed conduct
Arisen from the perfection of wisdom,
He is skilled in all the outer treatises:
The perfect and supreme master.
Endowed with the meditation of union,
And with a mind that is free of doubt,
One should eat every kind of food.
With the hundred syllables, everywhere,
Having firmly meditated on the secret mantra,
Joining it with one's own name,
Interweave them as one and recite.
Always recite the garland mantra as well,
And all aims will be accomplished.
Higher even than the highest,
The vow of the net of ḍākinīs.
With the collected secret mantras, the armor,
The essence and the near essence,
Protect with the collected secret mantras.
In a maṇḍala of utmost beauty,
Having well arranged the maṇḍala of letters,
Generate the heroes and the yoginīs.
This is all the secret mantras,
The sublime and supreme of the mātṛkās,
The secret, the joy, the hidden;
It is the compendium of all secret mantras,
It is the abode of all secret mantras.
The arising of beings, too, is pervaded by the mātṛkās.
This reality is not to be shown,
For that would destroy the siddhi.
This meditation is supreme,
The unsurpassed siddhi of what one desires.
A person, merely by meditating on this,
Attains the state of Vajrasattva.
He is clad in the armor of loving-kindness;
Compassion is the armor of Dharma;
He is equipped with wisdom as his weapon;
The māra of the afflictions is fully cast off.
Protected by Mahāpratyaṅgirā,
Marked by the four vajra faces,
Through the division of body, speech and mind
Marked by the vajra of the three joined:
This great protection, supreme of all,
Bestows every siddhi.
The wheel of command is a great protection;
Through the kīla, siddhi comes without obstructors.
The supreme protection of these
Bestows awakening on the practitioner.
With the fence of vajra kīlas,
The vajra ground and the net,
The tent above and the canopy,
Having driven out the vicious māras,
One does not kill; there is no killing.
For one who has tamed his own mind,
It is his own mind that is bound.
Who is there to be killed, and by whom?
The mind of equality is the scent;
The square is the anointing;
White sandalwood is the body:
It is abiding in equality itself.
The flowers, yogin, are patience,
And are said to be strewn with effort.
Natural purity is the incense;
The meditation of equality is the incense.

This concludes the third chapter, teaching the ultimate meaning of the essence of reality, from the Great Abhidhāna Tantra, the Highest.

Then the Blessed One Vajraḍāka spoke the secret of reality:

Now I shall properly explain
The Abhidhāna Tantra, highest of the high,
Just as the lineage of masters has explained it.
Having cast off outer conceptual thought,
The pledge of equality, supreme and sublime,
Is without conception and without analysis,
And all fears are fully cast off.
There are no lunar dates and no constellations,
Nor is there any rite of training.
There is no cleanliness and no uncleanness,
Nor are there any acts of purification.
It is free from times and measures,
And the conduct of purification is fully cast off.
Knowing the application of his own mantra and tantra,
He also works for the benefit of all sentient beings.
In mountain ravines and on mountain peaks,
On the banks of great rivers,
On the shores of great oceans,
At a solitary tree and at virtuous places,
In a house of the mātṛkās or a charnel ground,
In various excellent gardens,
In temples or at stūpas,
In houses or at crossroads:
If the practitioner accomplishes union there,
It grants the fruit of every desire.
Having examined the place,
Wherever the mind delights,
If the various sublime Saṃvara
Is practiced there, siddhi will come.
From mind that is free of any joining,
Through a meditation divided in many ways,
Having anointed the ground
With the nectar of the five wisdoms,
One should strew with effort
The flowers of the perfection of patience.
The delightful seat of the various mudrās
Should be made vast through meditative equipoise.
With a one-pointed mind,
Accomplish the perfection of meditative concentration.
Abiding in the four abodes of Brahmā,
Practice the four means of attraction.
Focusing as well on the ten virtues,
Work too for the benefit of all sentient beings.
When one looks outward and inward,
All phenomena are empty.
The supreme and sublime accumulation for awakening
Abides firmly in mind alone.
In the space at the center of one's heart,
Meditate on a sun disk resting there.
Upon it, that which is the supreme reality
Is the five wisdoms, the peaceful.
With bright rays of many kinds
Radiating, it pervades everywhere.
Having roused those who were accomplished before,
One also confers the blessing.
By delighting in meditating on the element of space,
One sees the supreme maṇḍala.
Like a pod filled with sesame seeds,
One sees it pervaded by deities and wrathful ones.
Having seen the buddhas and bodhisattvas,
The mind of awakening and the chief of masters,
And the various goddesses,
I shall make offerings and praise them.
Emanating buddhas, hosts of emanations,
The king of wrathful ones, with the yoginīs,
Establishes the sentient beings of the six classes of beings
In the state of buddhahood.
Having seen that supreme one, pervading
The whole expanse of space, and having gathered it in,
All go for refuge to the Three.
Until awakening is finally reached,
All sentient beings without exception
Are placed in that state of buddhahood.
To that the practitioner should pray:
"In that which has arisen from virtuous action,
I always go for refuge."
Pride, which has the form of delusion,
Form, feeling, perception,
Formations and consciousness likewise:

oṃ bruṃ aṃ hrīṃ ho hūṃ

These are pervaded by Vairocana,
Vajra Sūrya, Narteśvara
And Vajrarāja,
And likewise by supreme Vajrasattva.
All the tathāgatas
Are glorious Heruka Vajra.
Eye, ear and nose,
Tongue, awareness, and likewise mind:

oṃ hūṃ trāṃ āṃ khaṃ haṃ

Delusion and aversion,
Likewise jealousy and attachment,
Avarice and stupidity:
In all of the sense sources
They are the Lord Vajra himself.
Earth and water, and likewise fire,
Wind and space itself:

lāṃ māṃ pāṃ tāṃ khāṃ

Pātanī and Māraṇī,
Ākarṣaṇī and Narteśvarī,
And the goddess Padmajālinī:
Equal to space, like the sky.

Thus the aggregates, the elements
And the sense sources: the yogin
Should know the nature of ordinary things
To be completely pure.
All ordinary phenomena are completely pure,
And I too am completely pure.
Then go for refuge to the Three.
By turning into a body of mantra,
Having become glorious Heruka myself,
I also work for the benefit of all sentient beings.
Having attained the supreme state of Heruka,
May I place and establish in it
All sentient beings without exception.

oṃ svabhāvaśuddhāḥ sarvadharmāḥ svabhāvaśuddho'haṃ

This should be recited up to three times.
Into the insubstantial ten directions, the front and the rest,
Send forth the garland of the essence mantra.
Then, having seen that essence,
And likewise the net and the fence,
Bless the vajra ground and the rest.
The canopy and the net of arrows,
And all around, the blazing vajra fire:
Set these out far beyond.

oṃ medinīvajrībhava vajrabandha hūṃ oṃ vajraprākāra hūṃ vaṃ hūṃ oṃ vajrapañcara hūṃ vaṃ hūṃ oṃ vajravitāna hūṃ khaṃ hūṃ oṃ vajraśarajāla trāṃ śaṃ trāṃ oṃ vajrajvalānalārka hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ

The wheel of command of Heruka,
The eight chiefs, blazing:
Having seen them clearly,
Meditate on Vajrasattva
Dwelling in the center of a sun disk,
With three faces and six arms, peaceful,
Like pure crystal and the color of the moon,
Joined in equality with the great mudrā,
Seated well in cross-legged posture with his wisdom consort,
His head with a garland as of skulls,
And Akṣobhya dwelling as his crown ornament,
Complete with all the ornaments,
Marked with all the major marks,
And adorned with all the minor marks.
He holds a skull cup and a khaṭvāṃga,
Embraces her well with vajra and bell,
And holds a noose and a hook:
The great hero dwells in the state of bliss.
White, yellow and green:
The other two faces vary in color.
In forward and in reverse order
He sends forth his eight wrathful ones:
Vajra Raudra, nalārkaṃ,
And likewise Vajroṣṇīṣa,
Hearth Union, Vajrayakṣa,
Vajrakāla, Mahābala,
And the one called Vajrabhairava:
These wrathful ones are set in place.
Blue, yellow, and likewise red,
Green, and of the color of smoke,
White, black, and of saffron color;
Most terrifying, with crowns of skulls,
Three-faced and six-armed,
All ornamented with ornaments
Of human bone, and blazing fiercely,
With the right leg extended,
They dwell within variegated lotuses,
Dwelling in the center of sun disks.
Imagine the eight world protectors
Pressed down beneath their feet.
They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṃ,
And bear the supreme noose and hook as well,
All with bells and ḍamaru drums,
Well embraced in the bliss of wisdom.
Adorned with reddish-yellow hair,
Terrifying with bared fangs,
Wearing tiger skins as clothing,
Bearing ornaments of terrifying garlands:
With these, adorn yourself.
Meditate on Vajra Hūṃkāra:
Wrathful, blazing and moving,
Fierce and terrifying, transformed,
Well adorned with all the ornaments,
Standing in the posture with the right leg extended,
He bears a hook, a noose and a khaṭvāṃ,
And holds a bell, a vajra and a skull cup.
He wears a necklace of heads upon his body,
And a girdle of a garland of skulls;
All the vicious ones he presses beneath his feet;
He is joined in equality of means and wisdom,
And has Vajrasattva as his crown ornament.
One should meditate on Vajrabhairava.
Meditate on the two wrathful ones:
Above is Uṣṇīṣacakravartin;
Meditate on Vajrabhairava.
Below is Vajrapātāla,
Of white and blue color,
Holding a skull cup and a khaṭvāṃ,
Holding a wheel and a spear,
A noose, a hook, a bell and a vajra,
Standing in the posture with the right leg extended.
Having made the kīla into a wrathful one,
Send it forth into the ten directions.
The command given to it is:
"Swiftly make the obstructors nonexistent!"
Having recollected the secret mantra,
Plant them evenly in the four directions.
Joining the thumb and the threatening forefinger,
Snap the fingers toward the ten directions.

oṃ suṃbhani suṃbha hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
To the east.
oṃ gṛhṇa gṛhṇa hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
To the north.
oṃ gṛhṇāpaya gṛhṇāpaya gṛhṇāpaya hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
To the west.
oṃ ānaya hoḥ bhagvāna vajra hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
To the south.

With this king of secret mantras,
Into the brains of all the obstructors
Drive in the vajra kīla and press it down,
In forward and in reverse order.

oṃ gha gha ghātaya ghātaya sarvaduṣṭāṃ vighnān hūṃ phaṭ kīlaya kīlaya sarvapāpaṃ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ vajrakīlaya vajradhara ājñāpayati kāyavākcitta vajrakīlaya hūṃ phaṭ

Having looked upon the kīla as the vajra wrathful one,
One should examine it thoroughly.
In forward and in reverse order,
Strike it with the vajra of siddhi.

oṃ vajra hūṃ muṅgara vajrakīla ākoṭaya hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ

The wise one should fiercely recite
The words of the vajra wrathful ones, the words of secret mantra:

oṃ khavajradhṛk vajra hūṃkāra vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajracaṇḍa vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrānalārka vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajroṣṇīṣa vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrakuṇḍali vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrayakṣa vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrakarāla vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajramahābala vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrabhiṣana vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajroṣṇīṣacakravarti vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajrapātāla vajra hūṃ phaṭ

By accomplishing these commands,
At once there will be no obstructors.
māṃsa, marrow, fat and saliva,
śukra and rakta mixed together:
The yogin should make them into pills;
If he eats them, siddhi is certain.
Blue, yellow and green:
Having made oneself into Vajra Hūṃkāra,
Yellow, black and green,
Smoky, yellow and pale yellow,
White, yellow and green,
Red, yellow and green:
Thus the four, like the lord,
With Akṣobhya as crown ornament, possessing the pledge.
By meditating, one pleases the vajra holder.
In this way all the wrathful ones as well
Do not transgress the pledge.
Thus the command of the chief
Of the many and various should be taken up.
Wherever the yogin dwells,
He sees all obstructors as nonexistent.
Naked, or else not naked,
With hair unbound and limbs smeared with ashes,
Thus, for as long as he does not tire,
He should perform the recitation of the wheels.
The vajra of the wisdom of emptiness:
Recollect it with faith and rest in equipoise.

oṃ śūnyatājñānavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ oṃ animittajñānavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ oṃ apraṇihitajñānavajrasvabhāvaātmako'haṃ

Wisdom that is like a mirage
Should be examined as mind alone.
This is equal to space;
There is no dharma, nor is there any non-dharma.
Just as space has no stain,
It is without substance and without object.

That which abides, clear, in the center of the union at the heart,
Where the mind of subtle view and self-arisen experience enters,
Which approaches the three realms entirely as nondual:
To that clear and supreme dharmadhātu I bow.
That which binds firmly within it all the steeds, the movements of things,
Which has become the vessel where wisdom and all the victors gather,
Which approaches the three realms entirely as nondual:
Its vision, like space, has become stainless glory.

Through the yoga of recollecting the Buddha,
Meditate on the recollection of the Dharma.
Through the yoga of recollecting the Dharma,
The dharmakāya will arise of itself.
In the center is the illuminating consciousness.
For those who know the supreme wisdom,
Through the habit of thorough meditation,
This blazes forth without doubt.
Always abiding in the heart
Is the single drop, without letters.
If a person meditates on it,
Supreme wisdom will arise.
Through the yoga of recollecting the sattva,
Meditate on the recollection of the sattva.
Through the dharma of recollecting the sattva,
The Dharma sattva arises in particular.
I, who benefit beings with compassion,
See through the power of aspiration,
And having seen, as far as below,
In the center of a moon disk,
Vajrasattva, the omniscient one,
White and yellow and likewise red
And blue, with four faces.
Imagine the wind maṇḍala
Arising perfectly from the letter yaṃ,
Blue in color and shaped like a bow;
Meditate on it as marked with banners,
And visualize garlands of smoke pervading it.
Above it is the letter raṃ,
The supreme and sublime fire maṇḍala,
Triangular and red in color,
Pervaded by blazing garlands, blazing,
With boundless light like ten million suns;
Meditate on it as marked with vajras.
Above it is water itself,
Arisen from the supreme letter vaṃ,
Like the color of duśara (snow),
Round, with garlands of light rays.
Above it is the earth maṇḍala,
Arisen from laṃ, and yellow,
Square, and marked with vajras,
With yellow light rays massing everywhere.
Above it the yogin should meditate on
Sumeru, the supreme mountain,
Square, with the four jewels,
Beautified by eight peaks,
Arisen well from the letter suṃ,
Supreme and sublime in its rays and splendor.
Above it one should imagine
That which arises from a white bruṃ,
And upon that, together with the wheel,
Imagine a multi-storied palace,
Square, with four doors,
Adorned with four porticoes,
Beautiful with nets and half-nets,
Adorned with garlands of banners.
At all the corner sections,
And at the doors, the door recesses and the junctions,
It is adorned with vajra jewels.
Lay down the lines of the outer maṇḍala.

Within it is a wheel,
Eight-spoked and white in color.
At its center is a great lotus,
Variegated, with eight petals,
Its heart covered with anthers.
In the center of a moon disk
Is Vajrasattva together with his consort.
His four faces are white and yellow,
And likewise red and green:
Peaceful, wild, wrathful,
And with an expression most terrifying.
He is equally endowed with vajra and bell,
Spear, axe, and hand drum,
Curved knife, skull cup, and khaṭvāṅga.
He wears the skin of Brahmā as his garment,
And holds a noose and the head of Brahmā.
Thus he has twelve arms,
And sits in the vajra-hero posture.
His head ornament is a crown of skulls;
All his ornaments are complete,
And he is fully endowed with the major and minor marks.
He has the pledge of the wisdom of the mind;
His crown is Vajradhara.
Just as the means, so is the wisdom consort:
The five mudrās are blessed.
Divided by the distinction of the families,
The supreme sovereign of the vajra realm,
Through the method of union in great bliss,
Dissolves the two bodies into nonduality.
From sameness arise bodies:
The retinue of the ḍākas arises,
Together with the heroes and the yoginīs.
They stand with the right leg drawn in and the left extended,
Their faces white, blue, yellow, and red,
Their hands embracing with wheel and bell.
They hold, in order, the hand emblems
Of Vajrasattva and the rest, as before.
They are adorned with garlands of skulls,
And decked with garlands of freshly severed heads.
They bear the crescent moon,
Have four faces with bared fangs,
Three eyes, and twelve arms.

They trample underfoot Brahmā and the other gods, wear ornaments of human bone, have Vairocana as their head ornament, are adorned with the six mudrās, and wear tiger skins. He is embraced on his lap by Buddhaḍākinī, who embraces his body while holding a skull cup and a wheel, making the threatening gesture toward the wicked, and who entwines him with her two thighs, intoxicated with bliss and delight.

The elements, the aggregates, and the sense sources are blessed. The earth element is laṃ, Pātanī. The water element is maṃ, Māraṇī. The fire element is raṃ, Ākarṣaṇī. The wind element is yaṃ, Narteśvarī. The space element is khaṃ, Padmajālinī. Their colors are yellow, blue, red, smoky, and blue.

The aggregate of form is Vairocana, oṃ, white in color. The aggregate of feeling is Vajraratna, aṃ, the color of refined gold. Perception is hrī, Padmanarteśvara, red. Formations are Vajrarāja, ho, green. Consciousness is Vajrasattva, hūṃ, white. The suchness of all the tathāgatas is glorious Heruka, hi, black in color. The eye is bruṃ, the Vajra of Delusion, white. The ear is hūṃ, the Vajra of Aversion, black. The nose is khaṃ, the Vajra of Jealousy, yellow. Speech is the Vajra of Passion, a, red. Touch is the Vajra of Avarice, haṃ, green. All the sense sources are the Vajra of Lordship, haṃ, white. The body is hūṃ, white. Speech is āḥ, red. The mind is oṃ, blue. In this way one blesses and visualizes body, speech, and mind, all three.

Then, the ḍākinī of the eastern petal is blue in body. On the southern petal is Lāmā, green, that is, pale blue. On the western petal is Khaṇḍarohā, red. On the northern petal is Rūpiṇī, golden and pale yellow, with one face, four arms, and three eyes, her hair loose, clad in the directions, standing with the right leg extended, with a terrifying expression, adorned with the five mudrās, holding a skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, a hand drum, and a curved knife, her face full of passion, gazing and touching with delighted passion.

In the four intermediate directions, upon vases of the five nectars, one should place four vessels filled with bodhicitta.

In the east, in Pretapurī, are Mahābala and Cakravegā, black. In the north, in Gṛhadevatā, are Vajraratna and Khaṇḍarohā, yellow. In the west, in Saurāṣṭra, are Hayagrīva and Śauṇḍinī, red. In the south, in Suvarṇadvīpa, are Ākāśagarbha and Cakravarmiṇī, green. In the southeast, in Nagara, are glorious Heruka and Suvīrā, red. In the southwest, in Sindhu, are Padmanarteśvara and Mahābalā, moon-white and pale yellow. In the northwest, in Maru, are Vairocana and Cakravartinī, smoke-colored. In the northeast, in Kulutā, are Vajrasattva and Mahāvīryā, white.

The heroes, Mahābala and the rest, have one face and four arms and wear crowns of matted locks. With two hands they embrace while holding a wheel and a bell, and with the other hands they hold a khaṭvāṅga and a hand drum. They are adorned with the five mudrās and stand with the right leg extended.

The goddesses, Cakravegā and the rest, have one face and two arms, and embrace while holding a skull cup and a wheel with the threatening gesture. They have three eyes and fierce forms, their hair loose; naked, they abide evenly in nondual union. Clad in the directions, they are girded with a neck jewel, bracelets, armlets, earrings, the sacred thread, a necklace, a garland of heads, and a girdle of bells.

At the eastern, northern, western, and southern doors are Kākāsyā, Ulūkāsyā, Śvānāsyā, and Sūkarāsyā. In the southeast, southwest, northwest, and northeast are Yamadāhī, Yamadūtī, Yamadaṃṣṭrī, and Yamamathanī. Just as with the four ḍākinīs, so too are the distinctions of Kākāsyā and the rest. The goddesses of the intermediate directions have a twofold form and a captivating manner; they sit on seats of pretas, greatly fierce, acting for the benefit of sentient beings through compassion. All these heroes, moreover, bear a garland of wheels on their foreheads. These are the body wheel, which dwells beneath the earth.

The Blessed One, dwelling in the maṇḍala, gazing with eyes of passion at the heroes and yoginīs of every kind, sang:

"The bliss of manifold savors and essences,
The great bliss of passion,
Today I have found.
a la la ho, great delight!
This is great bliss itself.
The deity, the Sugata, is surpassingly beautiful."
Merely by singing this song,
Through joy one is freed from the states of existence.
The Sugata, the great purity,
Should be contemplated in the maṇḍala.
Some yogis wish the womb-born
To be brought under the sway of song.
The womb-born are the body wheel.
Having beheld the wisdom maṇḍala,
Having made offerings and praises, one should cause it to enter.
Through the practice of emanating and gathering back,
One should meditate on entity and non-entity.
The yogi, without error,
Should perform ten thousand or a hundred thousand recitations.
Then the body of one still subject to birth is accomplished,
And without doubt one will course through the sky.

oṃ śrī ka ya ḍā ka ru ru lu bruṃ hūṃ phaṭ ḍā ki nī jā la sambaraṃ svā hā. This is the heart mantra.

oṃ bhūṃ haṃ ho bhūṃ hūṃ phaṭ. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ buddha ḍā ki nī vai ro ca nī ye bhruṃ hūṃ phaṭ svā hā. This is the heart mantra.

oṃ bhruṃ sarbba buddha ḍū ki nī ye hūṃ bruṃ hūṃ phaṭ svā hā. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ kṣmā ma hā ba la kṣlāṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra be ge huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ khaṇḍa ro he huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ha ya grī bā hrī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ śau ṇḍi nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hūṃ ā kā śa garbha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra varmi ṇī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ki li śrī he ru ka ki li huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ su bī re huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ si li padma na rte śva ra si li huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ma hā ba le huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hi li vai ro ca nī hi li huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra varti nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ dhi li vajra sa tva dhi li huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ma hā bī rye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kā kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ u lū kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ śva nā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sū ka rā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma dā ḍhi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma daṃṣṭi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma ma tha ni huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ḍā ki nī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ lā me huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ khaṇḍa ro he huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ rū pi ṇī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

To the ḍākas, the ḍākinīs, and the rest,
First abiding upon the lotus,
One should make offerings and also recite the mantras.
Then the five natures will always
Be accomplished, and not otherwise.
Pacifying and subjugating,
Bewildering and suppressing
Are performed by the ḍākinī of body,
And the supreme accomplishment will be attained.
When the pledge beings have arisen,
One should meditate on the six limbs.
With the eye of pure wisdom
One should behold the heroes and yoginīs.

oṃ haḥ at the heart; namaḥ hi at the head; svā hā at the crown of the head; oṃ bauṣaṭ he is the armor; hūṃ hūṃ ho at the eyes; phaṭ haṃ is the weapon of all the limbs.

White and yellow and red,
Blue and green and smoky:
One should meditate with the mind on the six colors.

oṃ baṃ, Buddhaḍākinī, at the navel. haṃ yoṃ, Yāminī, at the heart. hriṃ moṃ, Mohanī, at the mouth. hriṃ hrīṃ, Sañcālinī, at the head. hūṃ hūṃ, Santrāsanī, at the crown of the head. phaṭ namaḥ, Caṇḍikā, the weapon of all the limbs.

All the heroes and yoginīs,
And the ḍākas, being seated,
Through the yoga of one's own deity
One should contemplate them all in this way.
Partaking of the five deathless wisdoms,
One should abide in bliss as one pleases.
Having worshiped those who hold the vajra pledge,
One should perform the approach practice at the four times.

This concludes the fourth chapter, the rite of the body vow, from the Abhidhāna, the Highest of Highest Tantras.

Then, furthermore, this is to be explained.
Having meditated on the maṇḍalas of speech,
In the center of the previous maṇḍala
One should meditate on the speech wheel,
Eight-spoked and red in color,
With its inner petals as before.
One should meditate on the speech ḍāka,
The transformation of Vajrasattva,
With four faces and twelve arms,
Abiding in the posture with the right leg extended.
Embraced by Padmaḍākinī,
He abides forever in great bliss.
He embraces her perfectly with lotus and bell.
He holds the head of Maheśvara (Śiva),
And with two hands he holds outstretched
The flayed skin of Maheśvara.

The rest will be as in the previous sequence.

The retinue of Maheśvara
Should be seen as trampled underfoot.
His faces are red and blue, likewise yellow,
And the left one white, each with three eyes.
He is adorned with crowns of matted locks,
His head made beautiful by a garland of skulls,
Bearing the crescent moon,
And having a crown of variegated lotuses.
His fangs are bared,
His faces terrifying in expression,
And he is endowed with the moods of grace and the rest.
He wears a tiger skin as his garment,
With a garland of freshly severed human heads.
He is adorned with necklace, bracelets, and earrings,
And with a garland of heads.
The sacred thread and the ash
Are renowned as the six mudrās.
These are the six perfections,
Sealed by the six mudrās.

Seated upon his lap is the Blessed Mother,
Padmaḍākinī, in her own color,
With one face, two arms, and three eyes,
Naked, her hair unbound,
Wearing a girdle adorned with pieces of bone.
Holding in her left hand a skull cup
Filled with the blood of wicked māras, she embraces him.
With the hand that holds a lotus, in every direction
She makes the threatening gesture at all the wicked.
Her splendor blazes like the fire at the end of the eon;
She ever delights in dripping blood,
And embraces him with her two thighs.
She is the embodiment of the compassion of great bliss,
Who bestows all accomplishments.
The Ḍākinī and the others explained before
Should be placed in their places in the directions of the lotus.
Their shapes and colors are as explained before;
However, the Ḍākinī is white,
And Khaṇḍarohā likewise is black;
The other two goddesses have the same colors as before.
In their left hands they hold khaṭvāṅga and skull cup,
And in their right, hand drum and curved knife.
In the four intermediate directions, too,
One should place, within vases, the five deathless nectars,
And vessels of bodhicitta as well.

In the east of the speech wheel, in Kāmarūpa, are Aṅkurika and Airāvatī, white. In the north, in Oḍra, are Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā, red. In the west, in Triśakuni, are Mahāvīra and Vāyuvegā, black. In the south, in Kosala, are Vajra Hūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī, pale blue like the sky. In the southeast, in Kaliṅga, are Subhadra and Śyāmā, pale yellow. In the southwest, in Lampāka, are Vajrabhadra and Subhadrā, white. In the northwest, in Kāñcī, are Mahābhairava and Hayakarṇā, green. In the northeast, in Himālaya, are Virūpākṣa and Khagānanā, pale blue like the sky. The speech wheel moves upon the earth.

The heroes, Aṅkurika and the rest, have one face, four arms, and three eyes, with a crown of matted locks and hanging silk streamers. With two hands they embrace while holding a lotus and a bell; in the left hand is a khaṭvāṅga and in the right a hand drum. They are adorned with the five mudrās and the rest, and stand in the posture with the right leg extended. The goddesses, Airāvatī and the rest, have two arms and one face. They embrace with the hand holding a skull cup and make the threatening gesture with the hand holding a lotus. They wear a necklace, bracelets, armlets, earrings, and the sacred thread.

Their necks are adorned with garlands of skulls,
And they wear girdles of ringing bells.
With the divisions of Kākāsyā and the rest,
As with the doorkeepers and others explained before,
The goddesses Yamadāhī and the rest
Should be known to be thus in the four intermediate directions.
All these heroes, moreover,
Have a garland of lotuses on their foreheads.
All the aggregates, sense sources, and elements
Should be contemplated as body, speech, and mind.
Following the rite explained before,
One should set out and meditate on the syllables.
The mind is āḥ, and it is red;
Speech is oṃ, said to be white;
The vajra body is hūṃ,
Like the moon in the sky.
Having joined and meditated on
All the maṇḍalas, every one,
And having seen the wisdom wheel,
One should cause it to enter through empowerment.
All having arisen and then dissolved,
One should meditate on and contemplate them all.
In the sequence of body, speech, and mind,
One should practice emanating and gathering back.

oṃ śrī bā ka a ā ro lig hūṃ phaṭ haṃ ḍā ki nī jā la saṃ ba raṃ svā hā. This is the heart mantra.

oṃ a ro lig hrīḥ hūṃ phaṭ. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ padma ḍā ki nī ye hrīḥ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svā hā. This is the heart mantra.

oṃ sarba padma ḍā ki nī ye a huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svā hā. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ ḍā ki nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ lā me huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ rū pi ṇī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ phaṭ aṅku ri ka huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ai rā ba tī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ da ha vajra ja ṭa li da ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ma hā bai ra ba huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ pa cam hā bī ra pa ca huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bā yu be ge huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bha kṣa vajra hūṃ kā ra bakṣa pa sa ru dhi ra antra mā lā ba la mbai ne huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sur bhakṣa ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ gṛhna su bha dra gṛhna sa pta pā tā la gha ta bhu jaṅga sarbaṃ bā tarjas ya tarja ya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ā kaḍ ḍhya vajra vajra bha dra ā ka ḍa ḍhya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ su bha dre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hrī ma hā bhai ra ba hrī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ha ya karṇe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ jṇauṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kha gā na ne huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kā kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ u lū kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ śvā nā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma dā ḍhi ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma dū ti ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma daṃṣṭi ṇī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma ma tha nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

Having worshiped with the vajra pledge,
One should thoroughly perform the rite of recitation.
Attracting and increasing,
And the various rites, will be accomplished.
One becomes a follower of the Dharma, and attractive,
And the accomplishment of speech will be attained.
The supreme, all the middling,
And all the lowest, abiding at the end:
In that one all abide,
The most sublime of those who tame the manifold.
Attached through the passion of desire,
Intoxicated with the joy of desire,
The great-souled practitioner
Abides in its power.
Abiding in the beautiful variegated lotus,
He practices the great yoga.
bhur bhūḥ, lord of the Dharma of passion,
Abides in great bliss.
At the time of rising, the pledge is this:
Partaking of excrement and vajra water,
And the aggregate of Dharma having been completed,
One accomplishes the state of a vidyā king and the rest.

oṃ haḥ at the heart; namaḥ hi at the head; svā hā hu ā ro lik at the crown of the head; bau ṣa ṭa he is the armor; hūṃ hūṃ ho at the eyes; phaṭ haṃ is the weapon.

Dharmavajra, Vairocana, Padmanarteśvara, Vajrabhairava, Vajra Sūrya, and Paramāśva: red, yellow, white, blue, red, saffron-colored, and green. These are their distinctions.

oṃ baṃ, Padmaḍākinī, at the navel. haṃḥ yoṃ, Padma Yāminī; hrīṃ moṃ, Padma Mohanī; hriṃ hrīṃ, Padma Sañcālinī; hūṃ hūṃ, Padma Santrāsanī; and phaṭ phaṭ haṃ, Padma Caṇḍikā: these are at the heart, the head, the crown of the head, and the eyes, and are the weapons of the Dharma. They are red, blue, white, yellow, green, and smoke-colored.

At the time of recitation they are set in place,
And one should practice in bliss as one pleases.
The heroes and the yoginīs
Abide in their own exalted stations.
Warmth arises through meditative equipoise;
It is to be taken up through one-pointed focus:
The sameness of the vajra of speech.
Through the yoga of one's own deity
One should thoroughly examine all phenomena.
Vajra water and Vairocana,
Honey and white butter, should be mixed together.
Through the yoga of the pledge of the tongue,
This is the very wisdom of the essence.
Having meditated on the lord as the head ornament,
One should eat one's food as one pleases.
Having taken on the form of the deity,
One is born as lord of the gods of desire.
Through food and drinks of milk,
Sickness and old age are cast off.
Urged on by this song of the goddess
Who is lovingly attached to beings,
One accomplishes the unsurpassed benefit of beings
In all the qualities of awakening.
Through the perfection of diligence
One generates compassion and teaches.
Having taught the secret Dharma,
One also attains the supreme accomplishment of speech.
For the benefit of all beings,
By teaching things just as they are, one is liberated.
Urged on by that very yoginī,
With wondrous pledge offerings
Of many and manifold kinds,
Having opened the eyes, one offers this praise:

ā pa ra ta ru su ra ta su khvā bā hā su kha me tva sau khya ma ya sa ma ya ra ma ra ma ho su ra ta sau khya ya tre sa ma ca ri taṃ su ga ta sānta ma ya pra bi kur ba bi kur ba su ra ta di ya padma ja le pra bi ba jaṃm pa sur tod su kha padma ja le pra bi nan tan ra ta su kha sa da na padma ja le pra bi marda marda su ra ta ma bi sa bi śva ja le ho ha hūṃ ha ha ho ja ya ma hā su kha su kha te je ma ya ra ma ra ma ho su ra ki tu su kha ba hūṃ bi ti dhe ja mu kra ja ya te ja ra ha na ta ho ba rā ha sa su kha ho hūṃ ho ho sur to tā rā ga su kha ra ti rī ji na pa ra ma śrīr ti ho pa ra ma he tu ma hā su kha ho su kha ma nu yo na ra pa ra me śva ra ho ho bha ga bān ma hā su khaṃ hūṃ ho ba hūṃ bi ti bi ti bi śva jā la ra ti bi śva ma ho su ra ti su khaṃ ga cid tra par ma ma hā su kha ho su kha ma nu gṛ hjya ho su ra ta ud ta ma san di su khaṃ a ā ro li ka a ma hā su kha gṛ hjya ho

Merely by singing these songs,
Joy and bliss are perfectly gathered,
And great bliss, as the mind desires,
And the accomplishments as well, are attained.
Having blessed the great host of the retinue,
The great maṇḍala is greatly delighted.
This is the vow of the lotus net.

This concludes the fifth chapter, on the vow of speech, from the Abhidhāna, the Highest Tantra.

Then, furthermore, this is to be explained:
The sublime maṇḍala of mind.
In the center of the multi-storied palace
One should set out the supreme wheel,
Blue in color and of great splendor,
Arisen from the secret, with eight spokes.
In its center is a lotus,
A variegated lotus with anthers,
Described as having eight petals,
And made into a pure seat.
In its center one should meditate on a moon.
There, pervading the moon,
Vajrasattva, the great bliss,
The supreme deity, is released and rises up:

oṃ hūṃ a ma hā su kha a la la ho ma hā su ra ta stvaṃ su ra ta su kha bi yo ga sur taṃ ud ta ma bo dhi su khaṃ su kha ma nu siṅ dha ho su ra ta ba ya sau khya ma ya oṃ āḥ hūṃ ho bha ga bāṃ ma hā su khaṃ ud ta ra ud tar sarba ud ta ma sau khyam ya hūṃ a la la ho ma hā su kha stvaṃ par ma su khā ma lai du bi bi ud ta ma jā la su khaṃ a la la ho su ra to sau khya ma ya

The Blessed One, the Great Bliss,
Rises up together with his retinue.
The deity of the mind wheel has four faces

And twelve arms, and is seated with the right leg extended and the left drawn in. He has three eyes and bared fangs, and his faces are blue, white, yellow, and red. He embraces with vajra and bell, holds the head of Viṣṇu in one hand, and with two other hands holds outstretched the fresh skin of Viṣṇu. The rest is the same as for the ḍāka of body. He wears a garland of skulls and a garland of heads, his girdle is bound with a garland of heads, and he has Vajrasattva as his head ornament.

He also bears the ornament of the crescent moon;
A crossed vajra surmounts his crown.
He wears a tiger skin as his garment,
And is adorned with the six mudrās.

The ḍākinī before him has one face, two arms, and three eyes, her hair loose. Naked, she is adorned with pieces of bone and wears a girdle. Her left hand, holding a skull cup, embraces him, and her right holds a vajra with the threatening gesture.

Her girdle resounds with the sound of bells,
And pleasing sounds of the Dharma
Come forth by the tens of millions.
It is the realm of phenomena, emptiness.
Sameness and discernment,
And the accomplishment of activity, are to be meditated on likewise.
This is the most sublime of unions.
Having well examined it as utterly pure,
Making sameness the single taste of the Dharma,
One should gaze upon the wheel.
On the eastern petal is the Ḍākinī,
And likewise in the south is Lāmā,
In the west Khaṇḍarohā,
And likewise in the north Rūpiṇī,
White and yellow and likewise red,
And green in color, supreme and sublime.
They hold skull cup and khaṭvāṅga,
And likewise hand drum and curved knife.
They have three eyes, their faces contorted,
Naked, with loose hair, terrifying,
And stand in the posture with the right leg extended.
Thoroughly intoxicated with the passion of desire,
With slightly bashful sidelong glances,
Full, swelling breasts, holding joy,
And the fangs of their mouths slightly bared,
They are sealed as well with the five mudrās.
In the four intermediate directions, one should set out
Vases filled with the nectar of wisdom,
And upon them one should place vessels
Completely filled with bodhicitta.
In the eastern direction of the mind wheel,

In Pullīramalaya, are Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā, white. In the north, in Jālandhara, are Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī, yellow. In the west, in Oḍiyāna, are Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī, red. In the south, in Arbuda, are Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra and Mahānāsā, green. In the southeast, in Godāvarī, are Suravairin and Vīramatī, sky-colored. In the southwest, in Rāmeśvara, are Amitābha and Kharvarī, whitish. In the northwest, in Devīkoṭa, are Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī, green and pearl-like. In the northeast, in Mālava, are Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā, lotus-colored and pale yellow. The mind wheel moves through the sky.

The heroes, Khaṇḍakapāla and the rest, have one face and four arms and wear crowns of matted locks. Wrathful out of compassion and terrifying, they embrace with two hands holding vajra and bell, and with two further hands, left and right, they hold a khaṭvāṅga and a hand drum. They are adorned with the five mudrās and stand with the right leg extended.

The goddesses, Pracaṇḍā and the rest, have one face and two arms, and embrace with the skull cup, making the threatening gesture. They have three eyes and pale yellow forms, their hair loose. The two forms are joined evenly in nonduality. Clad in the directions, they sport playfully. They wear a neck jewel, bracelets, armlets, earrings, the sacred thread, a necklace, a garland of heads, and a girdle of bells.

The goddesses of the doors and corners
Are Kākāsyā, Yamadāhī, and the rest.
Just as with the colors of the ḍākinīs,
So it is with Kākāsyā and the rest.
The goddesses of the intermediate directions are likewise:
They bear a twofold form and are captivating.
Having meditated on the hands and the rest in this way,
One is granted all accomplishments.
Having seen the wisdom wheel,
One makes offerings and praises and confers empowerment.
If one meditates one-pointedly,
One abides in the exalted state of Nairātmyā.
The essence, the wisdom pledge,
For the sake of all the practitioner desires,
Having arranged them in body, speech, and mind,
One emanates the supreme buddhas.
One should always keep the pledges,
And, with the nectar water of the five wisdoms,
Through the practice of the pledge offering,
One should recite according to the rite.

oṃ śrī citta ḍā kiḍhṛ ka a khaṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ ḍā ki nī jā la saṃ ba ra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ. This is the heart mantra.

oṃ hrīḥ h ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ vajra ḍā ki nī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ. This is the near-heart mantra.

oṃ sarba ḍā kinī ye vajra barṇḍa nī ye huṃ hūṃ svā hā. This is the heart and near-heart mantra.

oṃ ḍā ki nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ lā me huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ khaṇḍa ro he huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ rū pi ni huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ka ra khaṇḍa ka pā li na ka ra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bra caṇḍe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ku ru ma hā kaṅ kā la ku ru huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ canḍā kṣi ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bandha kaṃ kā la bandha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ pra bha ba tī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ trā sa ya bi ka ṭa daṃṣṭi ṇi trā sa ya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ma hā nā se huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kṣo bha ya su ra bai ri ṇa kṣo bha ya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bīr ma tī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ñrauṃ a mi tra bhā hrauṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kha rba rī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hraḥ vajra pra bhā hraḥ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ laṃ ke śva rī yi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ pheṃ vajra de ha hi pheṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ dru macchā ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kā kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ u lū kā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ śva nā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sū ka rā sye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma dā ḍhī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma dū tī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma daṃṣṭi ṇī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ya ma ma nī ye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

Having joined recitation with meditation,
Through the sequence of emanating and gathering back,
The mantra practitioner swiftly gains accomplishment
And attains the state of Vajrasattva.
The pledge of sameness is the supreme pledge.
In what is pervaded by the attainment of mind,
One practices the great yoga,
And attains the very power of coursing through the sky.
This one, throughout all the three realms,
Has truly entered the supreme state.
At the time of rising, for the one who holds the pledges,
The practice of the six limbs is taught.

oṃ haḥ at the heart: this is Vajrasattva, white. namaḥ hi at the head: this is Vairocana, yellow. svā hā hu at the crown of the head: this is Padmanarteśvara, moon-white and pale yellow. bau ṣa ṭa he is the armor: this is Heruka, sky-colored. hūṃ hūṃ ho at the eyes: this is Vajra Sūrya, like refined gold. phaṭ haṃ is the weapon of all the limbs: this is Paramāśva, green and pale yellow.

oṃ baṃ at the navel: this is Vajraḍākinī, sky-colored and pale yellow. haṃ yaṃ at the heart: Yāminī, white. hrīṃ moṃ at the mouth: this is Mohanī, red. hreṃ hrīṃ at the head: this is Sañcālinī, yellow. hūṃ hūṃ at the crown of the head: this is Santrāsanī, blue. phaṭ phaṭ is the weapon of all the limbs: this is Caṇḍikā, reddish yellow. One practices and abides in bliss as one pleases, according to the rite.

For the aspirations of countless beings,
This one, whose nature is the vajra of mind,
Is the infinite union of nonduality,
Abiding as the reality of all existence,
Stainless and without pride,
Beyond letters and beyond expression.

This concludes the sixth chapter, on the vow of mind and on service to the guru, from the Abhidhāna, the Highest Tantra.

Then, further, I shall explain
The supreme samaya of the maṇḍala,
Which bestows every secret of desire
And grants the most secret accomplishment.
For the benefit of the practitioner,
I shall explain the meaning of the practice.
In the center of the former maṇḍala
Draw a lotus with eight petals,
Multicolored and with its anthers,
Which bestows the fruit of all desires.
In its center is the syllable hūṃ;
Visualize Vajrasattva,
Fully complete with all the major marks,
Adorned with all the minor marks.
Through the union of great delight,
Supreme, nondual, without duality,
From the cause, the mind of awakening,
Comes the stainless moon of awakening.
Seeing that, the goddess rises
And urges him with this song:
hūṃ Play! Play! Delight, ho!
O vajra of great bliss,
From the very play of great bliss,
Please arise right here!
a la la ho Great delight!
You are the supreme of bliss;
Merely by kissing your mouth
I have found here the single taste.
Oh, Blessed One, delight in great bliss!
oṃ Vajrasattva, you who delight so well,
All tastes and the supreme tastes,
The great delight and the blisses,
And always all the accomplishments too:
Play! Play! with the goddess
And with all the yoginīs.
Blessed great Vajra,
Play in the goddess's delighted lotus!
hūṃ a la la ho Great delight!
Blessed One, you who delight in desire,
Grant well the accomplishment of desire;
Play with delight in the supreme lotus!
oṃ a la la ho Great delight!
With the bliss of the assembly, at all times,
Holding the supreme vajra,
In the lotus, the opened supreme mouth of the lotus,
Please play!
Just as the bee takes honey,
The deities play in the lotus itself.
Oh, friend, here in great delight
Join the supreme vajra in bliss;
Make bliss here at all times!
Today, at this very time, right here,
Is the gathering of perfect awakening.
a la la ho Great delight!
Great bliss, play in the supreme of all delights!
a la la ho Great delight!
Urged by these songs,
From sameness the glorious Blessed One,
The vajra of Saṃvara, arose.
Gazing upon the retinue,
The Blessed One is four-faced:

His faces are blue, yellow, red and green. He has three eyes and twelve arms. Standing with the right leg extended, he presses Mahābhairava and Kālarātri beneath his feet. With two hands holding vajra and bell, he embraces Samayavajravārāhī. Two other hands hold the skin of Gaṇapati. The third right hand holds a ḍamaru drum, the fourth an axe, the fifth a curved knife, and the sixth a vajra spear. The third left hand holds a vajra khaṭvāṅga, the fourth a skull cup filled with blood, the fifth a vajra noose, and the sixth the heads of the four māras.

He wears a crown of matted locks with ornaments,
Bears a garland of skulls upon his head,
Holds a half moon upon his head,
His head marked with a crossed vajra.
With terrifying fangs and a contorted face,
He is endowed with the moods, the erotic and the rest.
He wears a tiger skin as his garment
And bears a garland of human heads.
Necklace, bracelets and earrings,
And the jewel on the head adorn him;
With the sacred thread and the ashes,
These are renowned as the six mudrās.
In his lap is the Blessed Lady,
Samayavajravārāhī,
Who should be made with a body color like his,
One-faced and two-armed,
Two-eyed, with her hair hanging loose.

She is naked, adorned with a girdle and hip ornament. She embraces him with her left hand, which holds a skull cup filled with the blood of the māras. With her right, making the threatening gesture together with a vajra, she threatens the ten directions.

She has the splendor of the fire at the end of the eon,
Delights in blood, which drips from her mouth,
And embraces him evenly with both thighs.
She is of the nature of great compassion;
The abode of all phenomena, she is virtue.
Arisen from the vowels and consonants,
She is free of conceptual thought
And has abandoned both dharma and non-dharma.
By placing the goddesses on the lotus petals,
All accomplishments are granted.
The Ḍākinī, Lāmā,
Khaṇḍarohā and Rūpiṇī,
Placed in a circle turning from the left,
Grant accomplishment to the yogin.
Black, yellow, and likewise red,
And green, with fierce forms,
All are three-faced and six-armed,
Three-eyed, standing with the right leg extended,
Naked, with their hair hanging loose,
Pressing the four māras beneath their feet,
Intoxicated with passion and attachment,
With crown ornaments of skulls,
Adorned with the five mudrās.
Black, blue and green,
Extremely terrifying, with angry eyes;
Yellow, black and green,
Fully complete in the mood of compassion;
Red, black and green,
Surrounded by the moods, the erotic and the rest;
Green, black and yellow,
Gentle, and terrifying in wrath.
They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga;
On the left, a ḍamaru and a curved knife;
A head and a vajra spear on the right.
In the four intermediate directions, too,
Upon vases filled
With the water of the five nectars,
Place vessels filled with the mind of awakening.
The goddesses Kākāsyā and the rest
Stand with the right leg extended,
Black, dark blue, red, yellow and pale,
One-faced and four-armed,
Three-eyed, with fierce forms,
Adorned with hair hanging loose,
With crown ornaments of skull garlands,
Adorned with the five mudrās,
Naked, pressing pretas down as their seats,
Intoxicated with the compassion of desire.
In the left they hold a head and a khaṭvāṅga,
In the right a skull cup and a curved knife.
In the four intermediate directions, too,
Are the ḍākinīs Yamadāhī and the rest,
One-faced and four-armed,
Standing with the right leg extended,
Three-eyed, on seats of pretas,
With hair hanging loose, and most terrifying,
With crown ornaments of skull garlands,
Adorned with the five mudrās,
Naked, of various forms,
Intoxicated with joy and bliss.
In the left they hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
In the right a ḍamaru and a curved knife.
If they are placed in a circle turning from the left,
They will grant the accomplishments of the intermediate directions.
Kākāsyā and the rest, placed at the gates,
Should be placed in a circle turning from the right.
The elements, the sense sources and the aggregates
Visualize as body, speech and mind.
Through the application of the sequence explained before,
Place them according to the rite.
Of the maṇḍalas of Mahendra (earth), of water,
Of fire and of wind,
What is placed in their center
Are the four, earth and the rest.

Pātanī and Māraṇī,
Ākarṣaṇī and Nartakī:
These are the element of space itself;
The net of lotuses is emptiness.
Form is Vairocana;
Feeling is Vajra Sūrya;
Perception is the king Narteśvara;
Formations are Vajrarāja;
Consciousness is Vajrasattva;
All the sugatas are Heruka himself.
The eye is the vajra of delusion;
The ear is the vajra of aversion;
The nose is the vajra of envy;
The tongue is the vajra of passion;
The body is the vajra of avarice;
The sense sources are all the vajra of mastery.
hūṃ is the vajra of mind;
What is called āḥ becomes speech;
oṃ is the vajra of body;
he is vajra wisdom.
Through the application of the sequence, body and the rest,
Visualize all things.
The Blessed One is passion for bliss,
And likewise freedom from passion.
Having seen that wheel of wisdom,
Make offerings and praises, and confer empowerment.
The applications of subsequent passion,
By entering in, become the essence of sameness.
When one has visualized things and non-things
And performed the application of emanation and absorption,
With the offerings of the samaya of union
One should recite according to the rite.

oṃ śrī vajra he he ru ru kaṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ ḍākinī jāla sambara svāhā This is the heart mantra. aṃ hrīḥ h ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ This is the near-heart mantra. oṃ vajra vairocanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarva buddha ḍākinīye vajra varṇṇanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā These are the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra of Vārāhī. oṃ ḍākinī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ lāme huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ rūpiṇīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kākāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ ulūkāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ śvanāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ sūkarāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamadāye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamadūtīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamadaṃṣṭīṇīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamamathanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

Through the application of emanation and absorption
One should meditate the supreme meditation.
This is declared to be
The supreme secret of the yoginīs,
Fully complete in the sixteen digits,
Chief of all mantras and secret mantras.
Having risen from the session of meditation,
Set out the two armors.

oṃ ha Vajrasattva, at the heart. namaḥ hi Vairocana, at the head. svāhā hu Padmanarteśvara, at the crown of the head. gau ṣa ṭa he Heruka, the blood drinker, at the shoulders. hūṃ hūṃ ho Vajra Sūrya, at the eyes. phaṭ haṃ Paramāśva, the weapon of all the limbs. oṃ vaṃ Vajravārāhī, at the navel. haṃ yoṃ Yāminī, at the heart. hrīṃ maṃ Mohanī, at the tongue. hreṃ hrīṃ Sañcālinī, at the head. hūṃ hūṃ Santrāsanī, at the crown of the head. phaṭ haṃ Caṇḍikā, the weapon of all the limbs.

Thus things and non-things are equal;
All are of one taste as phenomena,
Abiding in sameness itself.
These five wisdoms of sameness
The yogin who keeps the samaya maintains.
At the four times, or else at the three times,
One should guard them as much as one is able.
This is the supreme secret;
It should not be taught to anyone at all.

This concludes the seventh chapter, the teaching of the samaya of the vow, from the great Abhidhāna, the Highest Tantra.

Then I shall explain according to the ritual text.
An eight-petaled lotus, beautifully fair,
With petals of various colors, delightful:
In the center of a sun maṇḍala
Visualize the king of lotuses.
At its hub is a crossed vajra,
A vajra with five prongs.
In the center of its hub
It is marked with a sun maṇḍala;
On that are the vowels and consonants,
And in the center, the tangible, empty.
Pervaded by the supreme Vajrasattva,
Oneself is glorious Heruka himself,
With five faces and twelve arms,
Three-eyed, transformed in wrath,
Standing with the right leg extended,
And likewise standing with the left leg extended.
Draw Bhairava and Kālarātri
Pressed beneath his feet.
He wears a tiger skin as his garment
And plays in compassionate wrath.
With crown ornaments of skull garlands,
He bears a crown of matted locks as ornament,
A half moon at the crown of his head,
His head pressed by a crossed vajra.
Endowed with the moods, the erotic and the rest,
He bears a garland of human heads,
Adorned with a garland of fresh skulls;
With necklace, bracelets and earrings,
His head adorned with a garland of skulls,
With the sacred thread and the ashes:
Visualize the six mudrās.
With two hands holding skull cup and bell
He embraces Vārāhī.
With the two other hands
He wears the fresh skin of Mahābhairava.

The third right hand brandishes a vajra and holds a triple banner, the fourth holds a five-pointed spear, the fifth an axe, and the sixth a vajra ḍamaru drum. The third left hand holds a vajra khaṭvāṅga, the fourth a vajra noose, the fifth a curved knife, and the sixth the head of Rudra. The Vārāhī abiding in his lap has the color of the Blessed One, two arms, one face and three eyes, with hair hanging loose. She is naked, with a girdle adorned with pieces of bone. With her left hand, holding a skull cup filled with the mind of awakening, she embraces him; with the threatening forefinger of her right hand she threatens all the wicked in every direction, and she holds a curved knife.

She has the splendor of the fire at the end of the eon,
Delights in blood, and drips with blood;
With both thighs she entwines him well.
She is the sacred one of supreme bliss.
Transformed into a fierce form,
Playing with the passion of compassion,
With Vajrasattva as his crown ornament,
Visualize glorious Heruka.
His faces are the color of the sky, yellow,
Red, green, and pale yellow;
Wrathful, laughing, and erotic,
Fierce, and endowed with peace.
The elements, the sense sources, the aggregates,
And the tangible are likewise meditated.
The Ḍākinī and likewise Lāmā,
Khaṇḍarohā and Rūpiṇī
Are four-armed and one-faced,
Three-eyed, with fierce forms,
Seated in the half cross-legged posture,
Supreme ones clothed in the directions.

With crown ornaments of skull garlands,
Adorned with hair hanging loose,
The goddesses bear the five mudrās
And bestow the liberation that is desired.
In the east, the south, the west,
And the north, set them out.
White and yellow, and likewise
Red and green, intoxicated with passion,
They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga
And bear a bell, a ḍamaru and a curved knife.
In the four intermediate directions, four vases
Fully filled with the five wisdoms;
Skull cups fully filled with milk
Should be set upon the vases.
Having arranged them in a circle turning from the left,
And having summoned the wisdom beings,
One should make offerings and praises and cause them to enter.
Having set them in mind, speech and body,
One should meditate on the supreme state.
Visualize hūṃ āḥ oṃ.
If one recites the secret mantra correctly,
All accomplishments are bestowed.

oṃ śri pajra he he ru ru kaṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ ḍākinī jāla sambara svāhā oṃ hriḥ h ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ These are the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra. oṃ vajra vairocanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarva buddha ḍākinīye vajra varṇṇanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā These are the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra of the goddess. oṃ ḍākinī huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ lāme huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ rūpiṇīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

Having sent forth the emanations of the wrathful ones
And brought the three kinds of beings to completion,
Endowed with meditation and recitation,
One should oneself emanate and absorb.
Throughout the three realms without exception
Enjoy body, speech and mind as one pleases.
Having risen from meditation, the one who keeps the samaya
Should put on the two armors.

oṃ haḥ Vajrasattva, at the heart. namaḥ hi Vairocana, at the head. svāhā hu Padmanarteśvara, at the crown of the head. pau ṣaṭ he glorious Heruka, the blood drinker, at the two shoulders. hūṃ hūṃ ho Vajra Sūrya, at the eyes. phaṭ haṃ Paramāśva, the armor of all the limbs. oṃ vaṃ Vajravārāhī, at the navel. haṃ oṃ Yāminī, at the heart. hrī moṃ Mohanī, at the mouth. hreṃ hrīṃ Sañcālinī, at the head. hūṃ hūṃ Santrāsanī, at the crown of the head. phaṭ phaṭ oṃ Caṇḍikā, the weapon of all the limbs.

This is the very essence of the samaya,
